### T05437a77d8a71ad26fbd0756

Text hash cbd736404c5666e3b22700180fba47f595d3775fa3a4de82f0fccb2d5b97da59; absolute characters 0–6399; files: jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_3969.txt

```text
Conicer'ning Costs and the Taxation Thereof in the District Court
and Magistrate Courts of the Panamna Canal zone, and Notices
in Probate and Guardianship Proceedings.
By virtue of the authority vested in me by the Panama Canal Act, approved
August 24, 1912, and. the Act of Congress amendatory thereto, approved Sep-
tember 21, 1922, 1 hereby establish and prescribe the following rules of practice
for the District Court and the Magistrate Courts of the Panama Canal Zone
with relation to. costs and the taxation thereof, and notices in probate and
guardianship proceedings : .. 3 I13
Section i. Section II of the Executive Order of January 9, 1920, is hereby
repealed, and the following is established and prescribed in lieu thereof :
" Section II. The United States of America, the Government of theCanal .one, and The Panama Canal, or any officer thereof who sues or is
in rfis official capacity, shall not be required to pay any costs forthe bringing or defending of an action. Every other plaintiff in a civilcase commenced in the District Court, except as hereinafter designated,
shall deposit with the Clerk or Assistant Clerk thereof a docket fee ofEight Dollars ($8.oo) upon the filing of the complaint. The defendant,or defendants, in said action making appearance therein shall deposit inhis or their behalf the sum of Two Dollars ($2.oo). An intervenor who 'is
allowe to intervene therein $ball deposit Five Dollars ($5.oo) upon the/4i~~ filing of the petition of inter> rtion. A plaintiff in a habeas corpus,
mandamus, certiorari, or pr ibition case shall deposit Three Dollars($3.oo) upon the filing of the cor; plaint, end a defendant therein, if a publicofficer, shall not be required to make a deposit for appearance therein.
Any other defendant shall make a deposit of Two Dollars ($2.oo) on enter-ing appearance in such action. Such sum or sums so deposited shall befull compensation for the Clerk or Assistant Clerk for all services performedin any action, except lawful copy fees for furnishing copies of any paper
or record therein. Any other plaintiff in a civil case commenced in aMa-gistrates Court at the time of, commencing the action shall deposit
a docket fee of Three Dollars ($3.00). The defendant, or defendants, whoappear in said action, or an intervenor who intervenes therein, shall deposita fee of One Dollar ($i .oo) at the time of appearance.. Such sum or. sumsso deposited shall be full compensation for all services of the Magistratein said action; except that lawful copy fees may be charged and collectedfor furnishing copies of any paper or record therein."
Section 2. Said Executive Order of January 9, 1920, is amended by adding
thereto the following sections, to wit :
"Section XV. The party in whose favor judgment is rendered in theDistrict Court and who claims his costs, must within five days after theverdict or notice of the decision of the Court deliver to the Clerk and tothe adverse party, or his attorney, a memorandum of the items of his costsand necessary disbursements paid or incurred in the action or proceeding,which memorandum must be verified by the oath of the party, or hisattorney or agent, or by the clerk of his attorney, stating that to the bestof his knowledge and belief the items are correct and that the disbursements
have been necessarily incurred in the action or proceeding. A party dis-satisfied with the costs so claimed may, within five days after notice offiling of the bill of costs, file a motion to have the same taxed by the Courtor the judge thereof in said action.
" Section XVI. The costs in the Magistrate Court shall be taxed bythe Magistrate without the filing and service of a memorandum of costs asprovided in the preceding Section, and upon such information as to Magis-
trate and Constable costs and other costs and fees and mileage of witnesses
as the Magistrate may require.
" Section XVII. The fees for the services of the Clerk or Assistant
Clerk of the District Court in probate and guardianship matters shall be as"follows:
Where the value of the estate does not exceed $xz,ooo ..... $3,00
XWhere the value of the estate exceeds $i ,ooo, and does not
exceed $5,ooo................ .................. 5.00
Where the value of the estate exceeds $,ooo............ 25.00

2
Such fees shall be in full of the services of the Clerk or Assistant Clerk insuch proceedings, except that they shall be entitled to charge lawful fees
for furnishing copies of papers and records therein. The Judge of the Dis-trict Court shall have the power, in his discretion, in any case where the
estate is small and the circumstances warrant, to waive the payment ofany fee to the Clerk or Assistant Clerk for services to be performed in such
proceedings.
"Section XVIII. It shall be lawful for the United States District
Court, or the judge thereof, to provide by order for posting in three public
places within the Division of the Canal zone~ where probate or guardianship
proceedings are begun or are pending, for a period of not less than ten days,
notice of any proceeding in any probate or guardianship matter, inc'nding
the probate of wills, the appointment of an executor, adniinistrat 4r, or
guardian, notice to creditors to file claims, the settlement of final accounts,
the distribution of property, and the discharge of executors, administrators,
or guardians, and to dispense with newspaper publication of notice in any
case. Proof of the pxoting of such notice, with a copy of the notice so
posted attached thereto, shall be made by affidavit by the person posting
the same. Where notice is given by publication, the proof of such publi-
cation may be established by the certificate of the Clerk or Assistant Clerk
of the Court. Such proof, in either case, shall be filed before any order is
made by the District Court in such proceeding to which such notice relates.
" Section XIX. The first two clauses of Section 531 of the Code of Civil
Procedure for the Canal zone, reading as follows:
"' For the complaint or answer, four dollars.
For his own attendance and that of his lawyers, down to and includ-
ing final judgment, ten dollars.',
and all laws, rules and regulations of the Canal zone in conflict herewith
are hereby repealed.
"Section XX. This Order shall take effect thirty (30) days from this
date."
CALVIN COOL IDGE
THr Wnm sHouse,
March 5, 1924.
[No. 3969.]
SOMANN "12wMearl"
```

### T08c83fceb944e7b79a4f322f

Text hash 350c1b30133ea346c76737b628a2fc5bd39b3e9a2fc7170a6cd1e279a2cb16fd; absolute characters 0–2172; files: jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_3973.txt

```text
Executive 0rber.
The Consular Regulations are hereby amended as follows:
Paragraph sob6Y2 is amended to read as follows :
5o634 . Additional compensation for vice consuls.-For such time as any
f ~1(C $) vice consul, whether de carriere or paid as a clerk, shall be lawfully authorized
Sto assume responsible charge of a consulate general or consulate during the
rXA absence of the principal officer at the post, he shall be entitled to receive in
addition to his regular salary or compensation as a subordinate consular officer
or employee, compensation equal to the difference between said salary or com-
pensation and fifty per centum of the salary provided by law for the principal
consular officer at the said post. (23 Comp. Dec., 77; 75 MS. Comp. Dec., 682;
i I Stat. L., 56, act of August 18, 1856, as amended by the act of March 2, 1909,
and February 5, 1915; 13 Comp. Dec., 247.) In case no principal officer shall
at the time be assigned to the post, the additional compensation shall be com-
puted on the basis of the salary of the principal officer last assigned to the post.
If during the period the vice consul is in charge a change is made in the prin-
cipal officer assigned to the post, the additional compensation of the vice consul
shall continue to be computed on the basis of the salary of the principal officer
last assigned to the post until the newly assigned pri ncipal officer enters upon
duty at the post. (Comp. Dec. February 29, 1924.) An honorary vice consul
who holds no subordinate position and is temporarily in charge of the consulate
is entitled only to compensation equal to fifty per centum of the salary of the
principal officer assigned to the post. (22 Comp. Dec., 145.) The foregoing
rules apply to the salary of the vice consul in charge after the death of the prin-
cipal officer. (24 Comp. Dec., 168.)
The foregoing rules are obviously inapplicable when the salary of the vice
consul as such or as a subordinate officer or employee exceeds fifty per centum
of the salary of the principal officer assigned to the consulate. (23 Comp.
Dec., 644.)
CALVIN COOLIDGE
THE WalTZ House,
March 19, 1924.
[No. 3973.]
I)
```

```text
Teporr
Executive cOrber
AN EXECUTIVE O1RER C.\NC1 LI.IJNG SALE FOF FROEHIACHI AND KUTTNER MADE
To COMPANIA MERCANTIT.DE ILIPINAS BY THE ALIEN PROPERTY CUs-
'rODIAN'S REPRESENTATIVE PU'RSU'ANT 'To THE "TRADING WITH THE
ENEMY ACT" AND AMENDMENTS ThllEoRE
By virtue of the authority v'estedI in me by "An Act to define, regulate
and punish trading with the enemy, and for other purposes", approved Octo-
ber 6, 1917, known as thec"''rading with the enemy Act", and~ the amendments
thereto embodied in" An Act making appropriations to supply urgent defi-
ciencies in appropriations for the lscal year ending June 30, 918, and prior
fiscal years, on account of war expenses, and for other purposes", approved
March 28th, 1918, anl all other oters and authorities me hereto enabing, I
hereby, in the public interest, make the following determination and order:
\VHEREAS, A. Mitchell Palmer.. ie Pro )ertv Custodian, has reported to
me that a certain property known as rohlich & Kuttner, was offered for
sale at Manila, in the Philippines, by ranicis Burton H-arrison, the Custodian's
representative in the Philippines, on May\Iavst, 1918, and the said property was
bid in by Compania Mercantil de P1ilipnas, on Miax' oth, 1918, and that the
said sale was not adlvertisedI or condnted in accordance with rules and regu-
lations prescribed by me, as required by the said "Trading with the enemy
Act", as amended, nor in accordance with the instructions and policies of A.
Mitchell Palmer, Alien Property Custodian, andI that lie has therefore dis-
approved the said sale;
Now, THEREFORE, I hereby (declare the said sale to be void and of no effect,
and I hereby authorize and direct the Alien Property Custodian to reject all
bids received at the said sale, including the bid of the said Compania Mercantil
de Filipinas.
My reasons for the foregoing (determination and order are:
(I) That by the powers granted me under said Act of Congress, and by
the Executive Order issued by me on October 1 2th, 1917, to the War Trade
Boad; and by the license grantedI by the said War Trade Board, the firm of
Froehilich and Kuttner should have been liquidated under said license and by
the party designated in said license, and not sold by the Alien Property Cus-
todian's representative.
(2) That, even if the Alien Property Custodian's representative had had
the power to sell and liquidate said firm, that not sufficient notice or adequate
opportunity was granted to purchasers to inform and prepare themselves to
bid.
(3) The exclusion of the public from the sale, which deprived it of the
public character required by the aforesaid Act of Congress as amended.
(4.) The advertised conditions of the sale excluded American corporations
who are legally entitled to bid, and contained several objectionable details.
(5) The sale was not made under the rules and regulations prescribed by
me and as required by said Act as amended, and not in accordance with the
instructions and policies of the Alien Property Custodian.
WOODROW WILSON
THE WHITE HOUSE,
13 September, 1918.
[No. 2954.]
```

### T141279e741dbcb434c0a701a

Text hash 0de8926acf1b210284eb73ecf514e3012fb882bff9e345c62a9640e78c4ef50e; absolute characters 0–20499; files: jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_4276.txt

```text
)Executive Orber
By virtue of the authority contained in Sec. 8, of the Panama Canal Act, as
amended by the Act of Congress approved September 21, 1922, it is'ordered;
1. That the following rules of practice and procedure for the United States
District Court of the Canal Zone shall be in force on and after the date hereof.
__2. That all rules of practice and procedure contained in the Codes of Criminal
and Civil Procedure of the Canal Zone, in conflict herewith, and the rules of practice
and procedure, numbered 1 to 23, inclusive, contained in the Executive Order of
December 30, 1921\are hereby repealed.
- RULES OF COURT
1
CHANGE OF VENUE
The district judge may order a change of venue in any case, civil or criminal,
from one division of said court to the other, whenever in his opinion, in the interest
of justice, such action becomes necessary. Such change of venue may be ordered
upon the motion of the judge, on the application of either party or by consent of
parties.
Whenever a change of venue has been ordered by the court, the clerk shall
immediately make out a true transcript of all the orders made in said cause, and
certify thereto under his official see [, and transmit the same with the original papers
in the case to the other division of the district, and the case shall be tried therein
as if it had been instituted there originally.
(Old Rule 2. L. C. Z. 19-197. Ses. 11 & 12, K. . 163)
2
COURT Doc~rs
The clerk, under the advice of the judge and the district attorney, may cause
to be prepared, and shall keep, all such dockets as may be necessary for the pur-
poses of said court.
(OldL Rule3)
3
MoTION DAYS AND DOCKETS
Saturday of each week shall be motion day in the Balboa division of said
court, and the first and third Mondays of each month shall be motion days in the
Cristobal division thereof.
Unless the court is engaged in the trial of a case, such motion days shall be
devoted to hearing motions, demurrers, entering decrees, assigning causes for trial,
confirming reports, entering default judgments, and any other matter ii the con-
duct and preparation of causes preliminary to or after trial.
All motions or other hearings not consented to must be made- in writing, filed
with the clerk, and by him noted upon the book known as the "Motion Docket".
Motions filed in the Balboa division, on or before Thursday of each week shall be
heard on the following Saturday, and motions filed in the Cristobal division on
or before Friday preceding the first and third Mondays of each month shall be
heard on the next following motion day, unless in any case the court orders such

2
hearing deferred; provided that any motion may be heard by consent of the parties
at the conclusion of the regular motion docket, or any motion may be heard at any
time upon the order of the court and upon such notice to the parties as the court
may direct.
(Old Rule 4)
4
PLACING MOTION ON DOCKET is NOTICE.
Any motion or demurrer filed with the clerk and by him placed upon the docket,
shall be notice to opposing counsel, and no further notice need be given except in
those instances where the law requires notice to be otherwise served.
(Old Rule 5)
5
How PLEADINGS AND MOTIONS MAY BE FILED
An answer may be filed in the clerk's office if presented within the time pre-
scribed by the rules or the Code of Civil Procedure. If the time shall have expired
within which an answer or any other pleading must be filed, it may be filed there-
after only by consent of the court. Any pleading which the court directs to be
filed within a given time may be filed in the clerk's office, if presented for filing
within the time prescribed.
Any motion or demurrer may be filed in the clerk's office unless there be some
order of the court or rule of procedure which prohibits the filing thereof, and all
pleadings or motions filed with the clerk must be noted of record by him.
(Old Rule 6)
6
PLEADINGS IN CIVIL ACTIONS
All pleadings and motions in civil cases shall be signed by the attorney or party
to the suit or his duly authorized agent or representative, and shall be endorsed on
the back thereof with the docket number, the name or names of the parties plaintiff
and defendant, the nature of the action, and the name of the attorney of record.
There shall be filed with the original of all motions and all pleadings other than
the complaint, a copy thereof for the adverse party, which shall be endorsed on the
back in like manner as the original; and it shall be the duty of the clerk to show the
date of the filing thereon and promptly deliver or mail such copy to the adverse
party or his attorney of record.
(Old Rule 8)
7
JuR Y
In the trial of any cause, criminal or civil, where a jury trial may be demanded,
if either party shall desire a jury, request theref or must be made at the time when
such cause is assigned for trial. At the trial each side shall be entitled to six per-
emptory challenges. A waiver of a challenge by either party shall preclude such
party, except by consent of court, from thereafter challenging peremptorily any
juror then in the jury box, and the remaining challenges of such party shall be
limited to jurors thereafter called.
(Old Rule 7)
8
EXCEPTIONS
a. An exception is an objection upon a matter of law to a decision made by a
court, tribunal, judge, or other judicial officer, in an action or proceeding. Except
as provided in subsection b. hereof, the exception must be taken at the. time the
decision is made. Rulings of the court upon minor discretionary matters, such as
adjournments, postponements of trials, and the like, shall not be subject to exception.
b. WHAT DEEMED EXCEPTED To: The verdict of the jury, the final decision
it an action or proceeding, an interlocutory order or decision finally determining

8
the rights of the parties or some of them, an order or decision from which an appeal
may be taken, an order sustaining or overruling a demurrer, granting or refusing a
motion to set aside an information, a motion in arrest of judgment, a motion for a
new trial, making or refusing to make an order after. judgment affecting any sub-
stantial rights of the parties, refusing to grant a change of the place of trial, allowing
or refusing to allow an amendment to a pleading, striking out a pleading or a portion
thereof, an order made upon ex parte application, an order or decision made in the
absence of a party, and instructions given or refused, are deemed to have been
excepted to.
(Old Rule 16; Code of Civ. Proc., Sec. 134. Cal. C. C. P., Sec. 646-647. Code of Crim,
Pro., L. C. Z., 206)
9
TIME FOR FILING BILL OF EXCEPTIONS
A bill of exceptions must be prepared, settled, and filed within ten days after
judgment is entered; provided, however, that the court or judge, for good cause
shown, upon application made within such time, may extend the time for preparing,
settling and filing such bill of exceptions, for a period not to exceed sixty days from
the time of making such order; provided, further, that an additional extension of
time beyond that granted by the court may be allowed on stipulation of parties.
(Old Rule 9. Code of Crim. Proc., L. C. Z., 206. Code Civ. Pro., Sec. 136)
10
MOTION FOR NEW TRIAL
A motion for a new trial shall be made only after verdict of the jury or decision
by the court, and before judgment. Such motion shall be filed within five days
after verdict of the jury or decision by the court, unless, for good cause shown,
the court or judge, within such five-day period, extends such time. Such motion
shall be in writing and must set out specifically the grounds upon which the same
is made. When a ground of a motion is the insufficiency of the evidence to justify
the verdict or decision, the motion must specify the particulars in which the evi-
dence is alleged to be insufficient. If a ground of the motion be error in law
occurring at the trial and excepted to by the moving party, the motion must
specify the particular errors upon which the party will rely, and in the case of a
question as to the admissibility of evidence the question, objection or motion,
ruling, and exception thereto must be fully set out. Such motion shall be heard
and determined as speedily as possible after the same is filed.
(Old Rule 10. Cal. C. C. P., Sec. 659)
11
MOTIONS AND ACTIONS TO VACATE OR SET ASIDE JUDGMENTS
A motion to vacate or set aside a judgment or order of the district court must
be in writing, and must be filed within ten days after such judgment or order was
entered except in the case of a default judgment. In the latter case th~e same
must be filed within the time and upon the terms prescribed in Section 106 of the
Code of Civil Procedure, which is hereby re-enacted and adopted by reference as
a part of these rules. Such motion must be accompanied by a showing that a
meritorious defense exists to the cause of action upon which the judgment is based.
Such a motion shall not operate in any case as a stay of execution unless the court
shall so order, or the party applying therefor shall execute a supersedeas bond to
be approved by the court.
An action to reverse, vacate and set aside the judgment of subordinate tribu-
nals, obtained by fraud, accident, mistake or excusable negligence, must be brought
within six months from the rendition of such judgment; otherwise, the procedure
therein as prescribed in Sections 141 to 145, inclusive, of the Code of Civil Proce-
dure, shall be followed, and such sections are hereby re-enacted and adopted by
reference as a part of these rules.
(Old Rule 15. Code Civ. Proc., Sees. 106, 141 to 145)

4
12
ARGUMENTS ON MOTIONS .
No argument of any motion shall be extended beyond thirty minutes to the
side without permission of the court.
(Old Rule 20)
13
TIME FOR MAKING DEFENSE
In all civil actions commenced in the district court, except divorce and
admiralty actions, where personal service has been had, the person served shall
make defense in court within ten days from the service of summons, and where
service by publication is had, within forty days from the last publication of
summons.
(Old Rule 18)
14
TIME WITHIN WicH COURT ORDERS MUST BE PREPARED
When a written order is taken in court, and the attorney for either side is
directed to prepare the order so taken, it shall be prepared and presented to the
court or judge for approval and delivered to the clerk not later than five days
thereafter.-
(Old Rule 19)
15
PREPARING COURT ORDERS
All orders, minutes of proceedings, or actions taken in court, and required to
be made matters of record, must be prepared and recorded not later than one week
from the date when made.
(Old Rule 23)
16
INFORMATIONS
Every offense of which the district court has jurisdiction must be prosecuted
by information signed and verified by the district attorney, or, in the case of his
absence or inability to act, by a~n assistant district attorney appointed by the
Governor of The Panama Canal. The verification must state that the information
is based upon due investigation of the facts relating to the crime charged therein.
All informations so prepared must be filed with the clerk. A criminal cause so
prosecuted shall stand for trial during the term in which the information is filed or
at the next succeeding term, after the apprehension of the defendant, unless for
good cause shown, a continuance thereof to a later date is ordered. Any such
information may be amended in matters of form; bu~t such amended information
must not charge a crime different from the one charged or sought to be charged in
the original information.
(C01i Rule 17. E. 0., p. 97, Code Crim. Pro., Secs., 3, 65, 94, 100. L. C. Z., pp. 173, 183,
187, 188)
17
BONDS TO BE RECORDED
All fiduciary bonds, attachment bonds, bonds required to be executed in all
matters of receivership, injunction proceedings, and any and all other bonds re-
quired by the Code of Civil Procedure, except bonds for cost, bonds for arrest,
or appeal from inferior courts, shall be copied in full and properly indexed by the
clerk in a book kept for that purpose, and such copy, duly authenticated by him,
shall have the force and effect of the original, and the cost of recording shall be
taxed as part of the cost in the court proceeding or action wherein filed, or in the
settlement thereof.
(Old Rule 11)

5U
18
ATTORNEYS ON BOND
Attorneys will not be accepted as sureties upon bonds or Tecognizaflees re-
quired to be filed in court.
(Old Rule 12)
19
BONDS AND COSTS
The Court may at any time before trial require a nonresident plaintiff to file
an undertaking for costs. The defendant may at any time move for security for
costs, and when the motion is made the nonresidence must be shown by affidavit,
unless it is admitted or it appears upon the papers or from the record.
(Old Rule 13)
20
BONDS
In all proceedings in which a bond is required to be given the clerk may accept
a cash deposit in the sum of the bond, a corporate surety, or a personal bond with
approved sureties. No corporate surety may be accepted which is not qualified to
do business in -the Canal Zone. At least one of the sureties on a personal bond
shall be a reieto h.Canal Zone. Where a cash bond is given, money deposited
with the clerk may be withdrawn only upon order of the court.
(Old Rule 14)
21
STENOGRAPHIC NOTES
Whenever, in the discretion of the court, the importance of the case demands,
the judge may cause the testimony in the case, or such part of the testimony as
he may deem proper, to be taken down in shorthand by the official court reporter
connected with the court and qualified to do such work, and the testimony so taken
shall not be transcribed unless so directed by the judge. Provided: Upon approval
of the court, any lawyer practicing before the court may have his own stenographer
in attendance for the purpose of reporting a case for his own personal use.
The original stenographic notes, taken by the court reporter, shall be a part of
the proceedings in the cause; and, unless such notes are filed pursuant to an order
of the judge, directing the stenographer to file the same with the clerk forthwith,
or within a specified time, the same must be carefully preserved by the court reporter
for at least twelve months after the final judgment of the court is rendered. th
Whenever the official court reporter takes down the testimony in a case,th
party making application for such work, except the Government, shall reimburse
to the Government of the Canal Zone the actual time of the court reporter while
thus engaged, no fractional part of a day less than a half day to be calculated;
unless for good and sufficient cause shown the court shall waive this charge.
A party to either a civil or criminal cause, who desires a transcript of the
stenographic notes of the testimony and proceedings therein, or a part thereof,
shall request the court reporter to furnish the same. The court reporter will
furnish to such party an estimate of the cost thereof, and such party, unless it be
the Government, shall deposit with the court reporter the estimated cost of such
transcript, and thereupon the court reporter must furnish such transcript with all
reasonable diligence, to the party requesting the same. The court reporter shall
charge the feesaloe for such service, and shall return to the party making such
deposit any unused portion thereof. The party requesting such transcript shall
be liable for any excess of such fees over such estimate, to be paid before delivery
of such transcript.
(Old Rule 21)

6
29
SUMMONS
The summons issuing from the Court shall read as follows:
UNITED STATES OF AMERICA,
CANAL ZONE
IN THE UNITED STATES DISTRICT COURT OF THE CANAL ZONE
DIVISION OF---------------
Plaintiff.
V.
Defendant.
You are hereby notified to enter your appearance in the office of the clerk of
the above-mentioned court, in writing, and make answer hereto, within ten (10)
days after the service of this summons and copy of the complaint which is hereto
attached, if service be had within the Canal Zone.
And, if served outside of the Canal Zone, you are notified to enter your appear-
ance within forty (40) days after service, and make answer thereto.
Upon the failure to appear within the time aforesaid, the plaintiff will take
judgment against you by default, and demand from the said court the relief applied
for in the complaint.
WITNESS the Honorable------------------ ----------
United States District Judge for the Canal Zone, this-----
day of --------- ,142- .
(Old Rule 22) Cek
23
PROCEDURE ON APPEAL FROM MAGISTRATE'S COURTS
a. In a civil action where an appeal has been perfected from a magistrate's
court to the district court, the party appealing shall pay to the clerk a docket fee
of Five Dollars ($5.00) on or before the first day of the next term of the district
court, or, if the record on appeal is not lodged ten days before the first day of the
next term, then such docket fee shall be paid within ten days after the record on
appeal is lodged with the clerk.. If such docket fee be not paid within the time
fixed, then the appellee may pa$'such fee, and on motion may have the same
judgment entered as that entered in the magistrate's court, plus the appellee's
costs in this court, or a trial of the case in the district court.
b. When such case is docketed the clerk shall notify the parties by mail of
such fact. If not disposed of as provided above by the rendition of judgment,
the plaintiff shall file a written complaint within five days after such notice is mailed,
together with a copy thereof for each defendant. The clerk must then forthwith
mail to each defendant, or his attorney of record, a copy thereof, and the defendant
shall have five days after such mailing to plead thereto. Where the defendant
files a cross complaint the same procedure shall be followed as provided above

7
upon the filing of the plaintiff's complaint. Such time for filing pleadings may be
extended by the court or judge. In framing the issues and in the trial and deter-
mination of such action on the merits, except as provided herein, the same procedure
shall be followed as in a case originally commenced in the district court.
c. Such action shall be tried and determined in the district court on the same
issues presented in the magistrate's court; provided, however, that where an appeal
is taken from a default judgment, the party defaulted may, upon timely application
to the district court and upon a clear showing of mistake, surprise, inadvertence,
or excusable neglect, be admitted to plead upon such terms as may be imposed.
No pleading or amendment thereto shall be allowed which will increase the recovery
beyond the jurisdiction of the magistrate's court. The issues in the magistrate's
court shall be determined from the record on appeal transmitted by the magistrate
to the clerk of this court.
(New and Code Civ. Pro., Sec. 68-10.5)
d. In criminal actions appealed from a magistrate's court, the affidavit may
be amended in this court as to matters of form or substance where the rights of
the defendant are not substantially prejudiced thereby; but the amended affidavit
may not charge a crime different from that charged, or sought to be charged, in
the original affidavit.
e. If it shall appear from the record on appeal or otherwise be made clearly
to appear to the satisfaction of the district court, that the transcript of a magis-
trate's court does not show all of the proceedings in said court required by law to
be shown therein, unless such defects be waived, the following proceedings may
be had : The district court shall forthwith notify the magistrate in writing of. the
precise matter to be considered and allow him to amend his return according to
the facts. If he neglects or refuses so to do, the district court may issue a rule
requiring such magistrate to appear and show cause why his return should not be
amended; but he shall, in no case, be required to amend his return unless it shall
clearly appear that the return does not show the proceedings according to the
facts.
(Code Civ. Pro., Sec. 71)
24
LIBERAL CONSTRUCTION-AUTHORITY OF JUDGE
The foregoing rules shall be construed to promote justice. Where the Codes
of Criminal or Civil Procedure or these rules fail to specify a procedure in any given
case or matter, the court or the judge may adopt and follow such procedure as will
insure the due administration of justice between the parties.
(New)
CALVIN COOLIDGE
THE WrITE HOUSE,
July 28, 1925.
[No. 4276]
CC133NU Y WSMThI!(Q aW
```

```text
/ S X
FEDERALREGISTER
VOLUM E 6 '9 3 4  ¿ y  
*  </A/ u e O  ^
NUM BER 248
Washington, Tuesday, December 23, 1941
The President
EXECUTIVE ORDER
Establishing the O ffice  of C ensorship
and P rescribing I ts F unctions  and
Duties
By virtue of the authority vested in me  
by the Constitution and the statutes of  
the United States, and particularly by  
section 303, Title III of the act of De­
cember 18, 1941, Public Law 354, 77th  
Congress, 1st Session, mid deeming that  
the public safety demands it, I hereby  
order as follows:
1. There is hereby established the O f­
fice of Censorship, at the head of which  
shall be a Director of Censorship. The  
Director of Censorship shall cause to be  
censored, in his absolute discretion, com­
munications by mail, cable, radio, or  
other means of transmission passing be­
tween the United States and any foreign  
country or which may be carried by any  
vessel or other means of transportation  
touching at any port, place, or Territory  
of the United States and bound to or  
from any foreign country, in accordance  
With such rules and regulations as the  
President shall from time to time pre­
scribe. The establishment of rules and  
regulations in addition to the provisions  
of this order shall not be a condition to  
the exercise of the powers herein granted  
or the censorship by this order directed^  
The scope of this order shall include all  
foreign countries except such as may  
hereafter be expressly excluded by regu­
lation.
2. There is hereby created a Censorship  
Policy Board, which shall consist of the  
Vice-President of the United States, the  
Secretary of the Treasury, the Secretary  
of War, the Attorney General, the Post­
master General, the Secretary of the  
Navy, the Director of the Office of Gov­
ernment Reports, and the Director of the  
Office of Facts and Figures. The Post­
master General shall act as Chairman of  
the Board. The Censorship Policy Board  
shall advise the Director of Censorship  
with respect to policy and the coordina­
tion and integration of the censorship  
herein directed.
3. The Director of Censorship shall es­
tablish a Censorship Operating Board,  
which shall consist of representatives of  
such departments and agencies of the  
Government as the Director shall specify.  
Each representative shall be designated  
by the head of the department or agency  
which he represents. The Censorship  
Operating Board shall, under the super­
vision of the Director, perform such  
duties with respect to operations as the  
Director shall determine.
4. The Director of the Censorship is au­
thorized to take all such measures as may  
be necessary or expedient to administer  
the powers hereby conferred, and, in addi­
tion to the utilization of existing per­
sonnel of any department or agency  
available therefor, to employ, or authorize  
the employment of, such additional per­
sonnel as he may deem requisite.
5. As used in this order the term  
“United States” shall be construed to  
include the Territories and possessions  
of the United States, including the Philip­
pine Islands.
Franklin  D  R oosevelt
T he W hite  H ouse,
Washington, D. C., Dec. 19,1941.
[No. 89851
[P. R. Doc. 41-9600; FUed, December 20, 1941;

ama C anal  T o I ncrease the C ompensa ­
```

```text
Executive Order 5880. July 9, 1932
Executive Order
DELIMITING Canal ZONE JUDICIAL DISTRICTS
SECTION I. By virtue of the authority vested in me by law, Section I of Execu-
tive Order No. 1898, of March 12, 1914 , relating to the Canal Zone judiciary , as amended
by Executive Orders No. 3965 , of February 26 , 1924, and No. 5704 , of September
2, 1931 , is hereby amended so that in addition to the area now included in the Balboa
Division of the District Court of the Canal Zone there shall be included in that
division the following additional area :
A strip of land 200 ft. in width , extending 62.5 ft. from the center line of the
Madden Road on its eastern boundary and 137.5 ft. from the center line of the Madden
Road on its western boundary, containing an area of 105.8 acres or 42.81 hectares ,
as shown on Panama Canal Drawing No. X-2005-33 , Sheet 1, Revised , the description
of the said center line being as follows :
Beginning at the intersection of the located center line of the Madden Road and
the Canal Zone -Republic of Panama 5-mile boundary line, said point being located
N. 29 ° 20′ W. a distance of 168.04 ft. along said boundary line from boundary monu-
ment No. 65 , the geodetic position of boundary monument No. 65 being latitude
N. 9 ° 07′ plus 3,948.8 ft. and longitude 79 ° 37′ plus 1,174.6 ft.; thence N. 43 ° 10 ′ E. a
distance of 541.1 ft. to station 324 plus 06.65 ft.; thence on a 3º curve to the left, a
distance of 347.2 ft. to station 327 plus 53.9 ft.; thence N. 32 ° 45′ E. , a distance of
656.8 ft . to station 334 plus 10.7 ft.; thence on a 3º curve to the left a distance of
455.55 ft. to station 338 plus 66.25 ft.; thence N. 19 ° 05 ′ E. a distance of 1,135.70
ft. to station 350 plus 01.95 ft.; thence on an 8º curve to the left a distance of 650.7
ft. to station 356 plus 52.7 ft.; thence N. 32 ° 58′ W. a distance of 636.0 ft. to station 362
plus 88.7 ft .; thence on a 10° curve to the right a distance of 227.3 ft. to station 365
plus 16.0 ft.; thence N. 10° 14′ W. a distance of 314.5 ft. to station 368 plus 30.5
ft.; thence on a 5º curve to the left a distance of 178.7 ft. to station 370 plus 09.2 ft.;
thence N. 19 ° 10 ' W. a distance of 4,250.1 ft. to station 412 plus 59 :3 ft.; thence on a
5º curve to the right a distance of 720.7 ft. to station 419 plus 80.0 ft.; thence N. 16 °
52 ' E. a distance of 1,664.3 ft. to station 436 plus 44.3 ft.; thence on a 5º curve to
the left a distance of 597.7 ft . to station 442 plus 42.0 ft.; thence N. 13° 01 ′ W. a dis-
tance of 543.8 ft. to station 447 plus 85.8 ft.; thence on a 5º curve to the right a dis-
tance of 770.7 ft. to station 455 plus 56.5 ft.; thence N. 25° 31 ′ E. a distance of 1,492.2
ft. to station 470 plus 48.7 ft.; thence on a 5º curve to the right a distance of 808.0
ft. to station 478 plus 56.7 ft.; thence N. 65 ° 55′ E. a distance of 281.8 ft. to station
481 plus 38.5 ft.; thence on an 8º curve to the left a distance of 446.4 ft. to station
485 plus 84.9 ft.; thence N. 30 ° 12′ E. a distance of 479.6 ft. to station 490 plus 64.5
ft.; thence on a 5º curve to the left a distance of 329.4 ft. to station 493 plus 93.9
ft .; thence N. 13 ° 44′ E. a distance of 1,639.9 ft. to station 510 plus 33.8 ft .; thence
on a 5º curve to the left a distance of 832.3 ft. to station 518 plus 66.1 ft.; thence
N. 27° 53 ′ W. a distance of 483.9 ft. to station 523 plus 50.0 ft.; thence on an 8º curve
to the right a distance of 469.6 ft. to station 528 plus 19.6 ft.; thence N. 9° 41 ′ E. a
distance of 1,697.6 ft. to station 545 plus 17.2 ft.; thence on a 10° curve to the left
a distance of 451.7 ft. to station 549 plus 68.9 ft., which is the intersection of the
Madden Road center line with the boundary line of the additional land acquired by
the United States on the left bank of the Rio Chagres in the vicinity of the Madden
Dam . (All bearings are true bearings .)
SECTION II . The area hereinabove described is that in which jurisdiction was
ceded to the Government of the United States by the Government of the Republic
of Panama, in Decree No. 43 of October 27, 1928, during the period of construction of
the Madden Dam .
SECTION III . This order shall take effect from and after the date hereof and shall
be in force so long as the Government of the United States exercises jurisdiction in
the area herein described .
THE WHITE HOUSE ,
HERBERT HOOVER
July 9, 1932 .
```

### T1ea171d4999257377cbc78a3

Text hash 61f73576f09db47065a3021e3078f3387726c2347e47fa528ad07bc25ac4bbb4; absolute characters 0–2103; files: jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_3968.txt

```text
executivee 0rbet
Relating to Pardons, Paroles, the Remission of Fines and
Forfeitures, and Kindred Subjects.
By virtue of the authority vested in me by Section 7 of the Act of Congress
approved August 24, 1912, known as " The Panama Canal Act," as amended by
the Act of Congress of September 21, 1922, I hereby establish the following
Order for the Canal Zone :'
Section i. The Goer of The Panama Canal is hereby empowered to
grant pardons, paroles, and reprieves for offenses against the laws, orders and ~
regulations of the Canal Zone, and to commute sentences and remit fines and' '
forfeitures. He may estab "sh by order a system for the pardon, parole, reprieve,
disposition, or treatment o prisoners, and alter or amend the same from time
to time.
Section 2. The Governor byore may establish regulations governing
the labor and employment of prisoners and provide awards for their good be-
havior, and may make regulations upon other matters affecting the welfare of
the prisoners and their management in the penal institutions of the Canal zone,
and may alter and amend such regulations from time to time.
Section 3. Where the Governor issues an order for parole of a person against
whom judgment of conviction has been given he shall specify therein the terms
and conditions upon which the parole is granted; and if at any time the Governor
is satisfied that such person has violated his parole the Governor shall have
power to revoke the order of parole and to order that such person shall be com-
mitted to serve the full term or unexpired term of the sentence imposed upon
him, even though the period of time covered by such sentence may have elapsed
before such revocation.
Section 4. The existing rules and regulations upon the subjects mentioned
in this Order shall continue in force until altered or amended by the Governor.
Section 5. All Executive Orders heretofore promulgated in conflict with
this Order are hereby repealed.
Section 6. This Order shall take effect thirty days after the date hereof.
CALVIN COOL IDGE
THE WHITE HousE,
March 5, x924.
[No. 3968.]
```

```text
(5)

UNITED STATES DISTRICT COURT
IN AND FOR THE CANAL ZONE
RULES OF COURT
to
Sessions of Court.
There shall be a session of the court held in the Balboa Division each month,
beginning on the first Tuesday; and there shall likewise be held a session each
month in the Cristobal Division, beginning on the third Monday.
Provided, however, the court may at any time, for good cause shown,
suspend a term of court, or order a special term in either Division.
2.
Terms of Court.
Fifteen days following the opening of a regular session in either Division
will constitute a term of court in and for such Division, but any cause pending
in one Division may, by consent of parties, or upon order of the court, be heard
in the other Division.
3.
Court Dockets.
In addition to the dockets prescribed by law, for the purpose of expediting
the business of the court, the Clerk, under the advice of the Judge, and the
District Attorney, may cause to be prepared such additional dockets as, in
their judgment, may be necessary for such purpose.
4.
Motion Days and Dockets.
There shall be established in the Balboa Division one day in each week for
the hearing of motions, entering decrees, assigning causes for trial, confirming
reports, taking default judgments, and generally any matter in the conduct
and preparation of causes, other than trials, which day, unless otherwise ordered
by the Court, shall be on Saturday, beginning at 9:13 a. in.
All motions, or other hearings, not consented to, must be made in writing,
filed with the Clerk, and by him noted upon a book to be known as the "Motion
Docket", before the close of the Clerk's office on Thursday preceding the Satur-
day on which such Motion is to be heard; provided, any motion may be heard
by consent at the conclusion of the regular Motion Docket, or by direction of
the Judge, if it be urgent.
There shall be likewise established a similar motion day in the Cristobal
Division to be held on the first and third Mondays in each month, and all motions
must be placed on the Motion Docket, in like manner, not later than Friday
preceding the Monday upon which it is to be heard,
" king Motion on Docket is Notice.
Any motion filed with the Clerk and by him placed upon the Motion Docket
shall be notice to opposing counsel, and no further notice need be given, except
in those instances Where the Code of Civil Procedure requires notice to be other-wise served. (7)r

8
How Pleadings and Motions May Bie Piled.
A demurrer to any pleading may be filed with the Clerk, and when so filed
shall be placed by the Clerk upon the Motion Docket for disposition at the
motion day next succeeding.
An answer may be filed in the Clerk', Office, if filed within the time pre-
scribed by the Code of Civil Procedure. If thc time within which an answer,
or any other pleading, must be filed shall have expired, it can be filed thereafter
only by consent of the Court, Any pleading which the Court directs to be
filed within a given time may be tiled in the Clerk's 0ffice, if filed within the
time prescribed.
Any motion may be filed in the Clerk's Office, unless there be some order
of Court or rule of procedure which prohibits the filing of such motion; and all
pleadings or motions filed with the Clerk must be noted of record by thim.
7.
Jury.
When in the trial of any cause, criminal or civil, either party shall desire
a jury, request therefor must be made at the time when such cause is assigned
for trial, and at the trial each side shall be entitled to six peremptory challenges.
8.
Pleadings in Civil Actions.
All complaints and pleadings in civil actions shall be signed by the attorney
or party to the suit, or his duly authorized agent or representative, and shall
be endorsed on the back thereof with the docket number, the name or names
of the parties plaintiff and defendant, the nature of the action, the return date
of the summons, and the name of the attorney of record,
There shall be filed with the original of all complaints and pleadings a copy
thereof for the adverse party, which said copy shall be endorsed on the back
in the same manner as the original.
9,
Time for Filing Bill of IExceptions,
All bills of exceptions must be prepared, settled and filed within the time
now fixed by the Codes of Criminal and Civil Procedure; provided, however,
that the Court for good cause shown, may extend such time for an additional
time beyond the expiration of the term, as in his judgment may appear proper;
provided further, that an additional extension of time beyond that granted by
the Court, may be allowed by agreement of all parties in interest,
to.
Motion for New Trial,
The application for a new trial shall be made to the Court during the term
in which the verdict or finding of the Court is rendered,
Such motion shall be in writing and must set out specifically the grounds
upon which such application is made, except that the party aggrieved may, at
the timne such verdict or finding of the Court is rendered, make oral announce-
ment in open court of hiis intention to file a motion for a new trial, and the
Court may, in its discretion, grant Sucth reasonable time, upon a proper showing,
within which such motion shall be reduced to writing and presented to the
Court, and thereupon such motion shall be filed bnn pro tubecias of thme date
of the oral announcement.

All motions for new trial should be made after the verdict or finding of the
issues by the Court, and must be made before final judgment has been rendered
therein.
And, if any question be raised in the motion for new trial as to the admissi-
bility of evidence, the question, ruling, and exception must be fully set out.
It.
Bonds to be Recorded.
All fiduciary bonds, attachment bonds, bonds required to be executed in
all matters of receivership, injunction proceedings, any and all other bonds
required by the Code of Civil Procedure, except bonds for cost, bonds for arrest,
or appeal from inferior courts, shall be copied in full and properly indexed by
the. Clerk in a book kept for that purpose, and such copy, duly authenticated
by him, shall have the force sand effect of the original, and the cost of recording
shall be taxed as part of the cost in any court proceeding, action, or settlement.
1 2.
Attorneys on Bonds.
Attorneys will not be accepted as sureties upon bonds or recognizance
required to be filed in court.
13,
Bonds and Costs.
The Court may at any time before trial require a nonresident plaintiff to
file an undertaking for costs. The defendant may at any time move for security
for costs, and when the motion is made the nonresidence must be shown by
affidavit, unless it is admitted, or it appears upon the papers or from the record.
14,
Bonds,
In all proceedings in court in which a bond is required to be given the
Clerk of the Court may accept a cash deposit in the sum of the bond, or a personal
bond, with approved security, may be given. Where a cash bond is given the
money deposited with the Clerk can be withdrawn only upon application to
and order from the Court.
Either the principal in a personal bond, or one of the sureties, if there be
more than one, shall be a resident of the Cantal Zone.
15.
Motion to Vacate or Set Aside Judgment.
All motions to vacate or set aside judgments must be in writing and signed
by the party applying therefor, or his attorney of record, and must be made
during the term at which the judgment is rendered.
Such motions shall not operate as a stay of execution, unless the Court for
good cause shown shall so order, or the party applying therefor shall execute a
supersedas with. good and sufficient security to be approved by the Court.
Exceptions During Trial.
If either party desires to save exceptions to the admissibility of testimony,
such objection, with the reason therefor, if required, must be made before
answer is made by the witness, and exception thereto be made at the time. A
failure to do this will deprive the party so failing to urge rtue admissibility of
such evidence as a ground for a new trial.

10
17.
piling Infornmations,
All informations shall be filed by the District Attorney not later than
Saturday prior to the regular term of the criminal session of court, in all felony
charges, where it can e done; provided, however, such information may befiled before trial during a regular session of the criminal term, or at a special
hearing by order of the Court,
z8.
Time for Making Defense.
In all actions where personal service has been had, the person served shall
I mwake defense in court within ten days from the service of the summons, andwhere service by publication is had, within forty days from the last publication
of service,
Iq
Time Within Which Court Orders Must Be Prepared.
When a written order is taken in court, and the attorney for either side is
directed to prepare the order so taken, it shall be prepared and presented to
the Clerk not later than five days thereafter, having been first presented to the
Judge of the court for approval,
20,
Argument of Motions.
No argument of any motion shall be extended beyond thirty minutes to
the side without permission of the Court,
21.
Stenographic Notes,
Whenever, in the discretion of the Cou'rt, the importance of the case demands,
the judge may cause the testimony in the case, or such part of the testimony
as lie may deem proper, to be taken down in shorthand by the official courtreporter connected with the court and qualified to do such work, and the testi4mony so taken shall not be transcribed untles so directed by the Judge. Pro.
vidd: upon approval of the Court, any lawyer practicing before the courtmay have his own stenographer in attendance for the purpose of reporting a
case for his own personal use.
The original stenographic notes, taken by the court reporter, shall be madea part of the proceedings int the cause; and, unless such notes are filed pursuant
to an order of the Jtudge, directing the stenographer to file forthwith with the
Clerk, or within a spec itie.d time, the original stenographic notes, the same mustbe carefully preserved by the court reporter for at least twelve months afterthe final judgment of the court is rendered,
Whenever the o1 1164 court reporter takes down the testimony in a case,
the party making application for such work, except it be the Govenment, shall
reimrburse to the Government of the Canal Zone the actual time of the court
reporter while thus engaged, no fractional part of a day less than a half day tobe calculated; unless for good and sufficient cause shown the Court shall waive
this charge.
The court reporter must furnish, upon request, with all reasonable dill,gentce, to the defendant in a criminal cases, or a party or his attorney it a civilcause, a transcript written out at length from his stenographic notes of thetestimony and proceedings, or a part thereof, upon payment by the personrequiring the same of the fees allowed the court reporter and prescribed by theJudcge

11
Summons~
The sum n$ sIsu~ing from the court should red a follow.:
VNI l T T its8 orAMEW1c4
y -Ca. zone-
IttK UNIT $ Sm R PK5tReCtCOURT or Arun CmMI , Zoi
SUMMONS
Dlefendtant
You are hereby notified to enter your appearance in the office of the Clerk of the
above mentioned Court, in writing, and make answer hereto, within ten (to) days after
the service of this summons nd copy of the complaint which it hereto attached, if service
be bad within the Canal Zone.
And, if served outside of the Canal Zone, you are notified to enter your appearanceforty (4o) day*siater service, and make answer therto9
Upon failure to appear within the time aforesaid, the plaintiff will take judgment
against you by default, and demand from the said Court the relief applied for in the
complaint .N 
Y ~'WITNEtIS the Honorable 414Mir 4,4A4
United States District Judge for the Canal Zone,
this 111M11 darnyof .81.1114419...4k
Clerk
Preparing Court Orderal
All order, minutes of proceedings, or actions taken in court, and required
to be made matters of record, must he prepared and ready for the signature of
the Judge of the court not later than one week from the date whens made.

ADMIRAL.TY CASES.
24.
General.
The rules promulgated by the Supremne Court of the United States as of
date of March 7, 5921t, governing pro eedlngs in admiralty, shall apply and be
in force in all admiralty proceedings ini this court.
a5,
Verification.
Pleadings in rem or in personam, claims and answers to interrogatories,
except on behalf of the United States, shall be verified by the oath or affirmation
of the party, or in his absence, by his agent, attorney in fact, or proctor. It
such verification be made by the agent, attorney in fact, or proctor, it shall
state the source of his knowledge or information; declare that the document
verified is true to the best of his knowledge, Information, and belief, and the
reason why the verification is not made by the party,
26,
Amendments.
Amendments, or supplementary matters, must be connected with the libel
or other pleadings by appropriate reference, without recapitulation or restate,
rent of the pleading amended or added to,
27.
Security for Costs.
No libel, petition, or answer shall be filed, except on the part of the United
iAt s, and in suits for seamen's wages, and suits in forma pauperis, unless the
Ph offering the same shall first file a bond or stipulation for costs, with two
ore sufficient sureties, one of whom shall reside or maintain a place of busi
ness within the Canal Zone, conditioned that the principal shall pay all costs
awarded against him by this court, and, lu case of appeal, by the appellate court.
In suits in personam, sucht bond or stipulation shall be in the sum of $;oo;
in suits in rem, or where process of foreign attacliireint is to be issued, the bond
or stipulation shall be in the sum of $250.
Salvors coming into port in possession o& the propcvty libeled, petitioners
for money in the Registry of the Court, and The Panama Canal, shall not be
required to give such security,
'Bonds and S ti pulstiratsa.
Unless otherwise ordered and approved by the Court, all bonds and stipuw
lations for costs, for the discharge of property arrested, for stay of eascutioni
of any process in rem, or in any other case wherein a bond or stipulation Is
required of the party appearing in the sit, under the satute or the admiralty
or equity rules of the court, Shall be executed and delivered by the party with
two or more sufficient sureties, one of whom shall be a resident or maintain an
office and place of business within the Canal zone, conditioned, to answer the
final decree of the Court, whether such decree shall be rendered in the original
or the appellate court,
tn lieu of sureties, the party may file his stipulation condition ed as above
set forth, accompanied by the deposit of cash, or the manager's cheek of a solvent
bank, or United States bonds ini the amount or sunt required, the same to bedelivered to the Marshal, or deposited in the R4egistry of the Court, as the
exigency of the case may demand, and such cash, manager's check, or United

States bonds shall be subject to all money demands of parties to the suit, as may
be determined by the final decree of the court; Provided, the bond of any
responsible surety company in w *if United States, and acceptable to the Court,
may be taken in lieu of the security and surety above mentioned, on the condition
that the bond so given and taken shall waive all questions as to the jurisdiction
of the court to enter judgment in any proceedings wherein said bond shall
have been given,
29.
Return Day of Process,
Original process on libels and information shall be made returnable on the
first Monday of each month at a stated or special term; provided, that twenty
running days have elapsed since the last. publication of a monition or the service
of process; and in cases where such delay of twenty days shall not have expired
on the first Monday of the month, said process shall be returnable on the third
Monday of the same month,
Upon process in remt, the return shall state the day of seizure or of sale, as
the case me Pr be.
30.
Release of Property on Bond.
The Marshal shall stay execution of any warrant of arrest or other process
in rem, or discharge the property arrested, upon receiving from the claimant a
bond or stipulation for double theta mount claimed by libellant, with sufficient
surety, approved by the judge, or, in hiis absence, by the Marshal, conditioned
to answer the decree of the Court in the cause, as provided in Section 941 of
the United States Revised Statutes, as amended by the Act of Congress approved
March 3, 189o (30 Statutes, 1354), The bond so taken shall run in favor of the
Marshal for the benefit of all parties in interest, and shall be immediately deliv-
ered by the Marshal to the Clerk of the court, who shall file the same in the
record.
But the parties to the suit may, by written agreement filed in the cause,
consent that the stipulation shall be reduced to such an amount as they may deem
sufficient to secure the claim of the libellant and intervenors and costs, without
in any manner impairing its validity.
In salvage cases the amount of the release bond shall be fixed by the Court
or by agreement between the proctors for the libellant and claimant.
3',
Amount to be Stated.
In all suits, except for salvage, the amount claimed by the libellant or
intervenors must be specifically stated in their pleadings.
32,
Clerk to Issue Process.
The Clerk or Assistant Clerk of the court is authorized to issue all admiralty
and other process without any special order, except in cases where the statute
or the admiralty or equity rules require an order of the Court, or of the judge,
to authorize process. He may also issue all writs of dedimus potestatent and
commissions to take depositions of absent witnesses, as authorized by the Act
of Congress approved March 9, '892, but shall issue letters rogatory only when
ordered by the Court.
33.
Mesne Process.
Mesne process may be either in personain or in rein or both.
Process in personanm may be:
(i) A simple citation in the nature of a summons to appear and
answer to the suit.
6

j 14
(2) Such a citation, with a clause therein that if the respondent
cannot be found, his goods and chattels to the amount sued for be
attached,
(3) Such a citation and attachment, together with a clause of
foreign attachment of the respondent's property and credits to the
amount sued for in the hands of garnishees named therein.
The names of the garnishees and the specific property in their
hands, if known, shall be stated in the libel or petition and in the
process, and the garnishees shall be cited to appear and answer on oath.
(4) A warrant of arrest of the person shll issue only upon the
special order of the Court~ alone, or with an attachment,
In suits in personam, except on a libel for liquidated damages not
exceeding $500, no process of attachment or foreign attachment shall
issue, unless allowed by special order of the Court, upon due proof of
the demand and of the propriety of the attachment being first made.
Process in rem shall be:
(i) By a warrant to arrest the ship, goods, or other things to be
arrested, with a monition to all persons interested therein, and shall
be issued as, of course, by the Clerk of the court upon the filing of the
libel and security for costs.
34.
Division of Docket Fee and Costs.
In cases for salvage, when several parties are entitled to participate therein,
the docket fee and costs taxable by statute in favor of the prevailing party shall
be divided equally among the proctors of the respective parties who share in
the salvage.
35.
ibels-When to Stand as Interventions,
When property is already under seizure by process of this court, all libels
filed against the same property shall stand and be considered as interventions
in the original case, and but one proctor's fee shall be allowed, which shall go
to the proctor in the original cause. The Clerk shall be authorized to make all
orders permitting interventions to be filed in all proceedings in rem.
36.
Payment Into Court,
In all actions for sums certain, the claimant or respondent may pay into
court the amount claimed in the libel, with accrued interest and costs, and may
thereupon have an order entered, instanter, for the restoration of his property.
37,
Stipulations on Intervening Libels,
Any third person who shall intervene in any cause of admiralty, and mari-time jurisdiction in rem shall be required to give a stipulation, with surety in
the sum of $25, to abide by the final (decree rendered in the cause, and pay all
such costs, expenses and damages as shall be awarded by the Court upon final
decree, whether it is rendered in the original or appellate court, and such inter-
venor shall also deposit the sum of Sio with the Clerk to secure his costs.
38.
Deposits for Costs.
Neither the Clerk nor the Marshal shall be required to issue or serve any
process, or to perform any service for parties, until their costs and the necessary
expenses incident to the service shall be paid or secured to their satisfaction,
except in cases of seanmen', wages and suits in fornma pauperis.

15
39.
Publication of Process,
Upon any seizure in suits in rem, or upon any information in rem or in
personam wherein publication is required by law, such notice shall be published
once unless the Court by special order, directs other or further publication.
Publication hereunder, unless otherwise ordered, shall be at least one week
before the date on which the process is made returnable. The published notice
need contain only the title of the suit, the nature of the cause of action, the
amount demanded, the time and place of the return of monition and the names
of the Marshal and proctor, and shall direct all persons interested to appear or
that default and condemnation will be ordered.
40.
Publication Notice of Sale.
Public notice of the sale of property after condemnation in suits ini rem,
shall be made by publication in a newspaper of general circulation within the
Canal Zone, for at least six successive days, unless otherwise ordered in the
decree, t «: last; publication thereof to be at least fifteen days before the date of
said sale, as provided in U, S. Revised Statutes, section 939. (U, S. Comp.
Statl, 1918, Sec. xg65,)
4'.
Testimony to be taken before a Commissioner.
In all cases of admiralty and maritime jurisdiction the evidence of witnesses
present at the place where the court is held shall be taken orally before a Corn-
missioner, upon at least cue day's notice to the opposite party; provided, that
either side may set the caso for trial in open court on the question of liability,
in which event all witnesses within the jurisdiction will be heard orally. The
stenographer employed shall W~ entitled to receive twenty-five cents per hundred
words as compensation for his services, and shall furnish one copy of the evidence
to the proctor for the opposite party, without charge, when requested.
42.
Evidence by Written Interrogatories,
Evidence taken under dedimnus potestateml, or under commission as pro-
vided in the Act of Congress approved March 9, 182, shall be by written inter-
rogatories propounded to the witness, filed in the Clerk's office, and a certified
copy of the same shall be served on the opposite party, together with a notice
by the Clerk that he is required to file cross-interrogatories, if any he has, within
five days from a date to be specified in such notice.
In case the opposite party is absent from the Canal Zone and not repre-sented by an attorney, service on the interrogatories and notice to cross may be
made by posting the same at the court house for the period of five days.
At the expiration of the time so allowed the Clerk, or Assistant Clerk, shall
issue the proper writ or commission.
43.
Time for Taking Evidence.
After issue joined, the libellant shall have sixty days in which to take his
evidence, and thereafter the claimant or respondent shall have the like time to
take his evidence, and no more, unless for cause shown the Court shall enlarge
or restrict the time, and provided the case is not heard in open court.
44"
Notice to Set Cause for Trial,
After the expiration of the time for taking evidence has expired, either
party may set the cause down for trial on motion upon five days' notice, served

16
on the adverse party or his proctor; but, in case the adverse party be absent
and not represented, the notice may be served by posting the cause for trial at
the court house for the period of five days.
45.
Certificate of Counsel, and Affidavit to Exceptions, etc.
Every exception or plea to at libel or intervening libel shall be accompanied
by a certificate of counsel that, in hit opinion, it is well funded in law, and be
supported by affidavit of the party or his proctor that it is not interposed for
delay, aind that the facts stated therein ate true to the best of his knowledge,
infor matron and belief.
46,
Intervening Libels,
No intervening libel setting up a claiui either or in addition to the amount
claimed in the original libel, whereby the liability of the surety on the release
bond would be increased, shall be allowed in any case after the property seized
has been released.
47,
Trial on Libel and Answer.
The libellant shall have the right, upon the coming in of the answer, to set
the cause down for trial on the libel and answer in all cases where the facts are
substantially admtitted, and nothing remains but que~stionx of law for decision.
In such cases the avermnents of the answer, well pleaded, except avermnents of
law, shall, for the purpose of the trial, be taken as true.
48.
Interventions in Remn.
Iuttrventionas filed in proceedings in rem shall be considered as denied, and
neted not be answered, but parties whose interts are itltolved mtay, at theft
option, plead, answer or demur thereto.
49.
Third Patty Practice.
If a defendant shall, by petition on oath, filed before answer, or within
such further time as the Court may allow, allege fault in any other party, in
respect of the matters complained of in the libel, or shall allege that he is entitled
to contribution or indemnity from any other party in respect of such matters,
and shall pray that such other party be brought into the suit as a party defendatt
in analogy with the provisions of Admiralty Rule 57 of the Supreme Court of
the United States, process on such petition may be issued and the cause shall
proceed otherwise as 'in cases under the 57th Rule.
30.
Note of Evidence-Testimony to be Bound, etc.
Before the final hearing each party shall file with the Clerk a written list
of the testimony and proofs on which fie wishes to be heard, with all such testi-mnony and proofs, and no testimony and proofs not so listed and filed shall be
considered by the Court or be included in the record on appeal. The testimony
shall be boud, consecutively paged, and indexed. No ease shall be set down
for argument or final hearing until this Rule has been complied with.
53.
Objections to Evidence.
Nor bectiou to the admissbiity of evidence taken wnder Rule 41 shall he
scored mart spoled at the time the evidence is taken, nor shall any ob ee-

17
tions to interrogatories propounded to witnesses examined under commission
be considered unless made by the opposite party at the time cross-interrogatories
are filed or before the commission issued; and parties making objection or
objections to the manner and form in which a commission has been executed
shall do so by motion to suppress.
52.
Confirmation of Commissioners' Reports.
Exceptions to Commissioners' Reports shall be filed within twenty days
after the date of the filing of the Report. Should no exceptions or opposition
be filed within said delay, the report may be confirmed on motion of counsel.
53.
Matters Not Specifically Provided For.
In any matter not provided for in the general admiralty rules of the Supreme
Court of the United States or in these rules, the rules of practice in admiralty
in the United States District Courts of the Southern District of New York shall
be made applicable and apply in this court.
```

### T4a71d8adda6b0af53295b050

Text hash 12bc0fb18dc3c94959772095c68c5b18f8c8ceaea318f74b628f80e7716493f4; absolute characters 0–1048; files: jobs/csc-annual-reports/output/texts/EO_4721.txt

```text
September 14, 1927.
By virtue of the authority vested in me by the Panama Canal act, approved
August 24, 1912, it is hereby ordered that:
I. The Governor of the Panama Canal is hereby authorized, as an excep-
tion to the provisions of paragraphs 6 and 20 of the Executive order of
February 2, 1914, as amended by the Executive order of February 20, 1920,
to increase above the limit of $960 per annum the pay of not to exceed one
hundred (100) alien employees in the positions they occupy under the
Panama Canal and Panama Railroad Co. when such employees by long and
efficient service have rendered themselves of greater value to those organi-
zations than can be adequately compensated by the limit heretofore
established.
II. Alien employees whose pay, as provided in paragraph I hereof, may
be increased above the established limit of $960 per annum, shall enjoy the
same leave and other privileges, and no more, as are accorded alien em-
ployees whose pay does not exceed this limit.
III. This order shall be effective on and after this date.

```

```text
FEDERALREGISTER
VOLUME 7 1934 ^
^ÜNlTtO  ^
NUMBER 99
Washington, Thursday, May 21, 1942
The President
EXECUTIVE ORDER 9164
Amending  S ection  1 of E xecutive  O rder 
No. 8986 of December 19,1941, Author ­
izing  the  G overnor of the  P anama 
Canal to I ncrease the  C ompensation  
of C ertain  E mployees
By virtue of the authority vested in 
Die by section 81 of title 2 of the Canal 
Zone Code, as amended by section 3 of 
the act of July 9,1937 (50 Stat. 487), sec­
tion 1  of Executive Order No. 8986 of 
December 19, 1941,1  is hereby amended 
to read as follows:
“Section  1. The Governor of The 
Panama Canal is authorized to increase 
the rate of compensation of alien em­
ployees of The Panama Canal or the 
Panama Railroad Company to more than 
$960 a year or 40 cents an hour; but 
such employees shall have no greater 
leave privileges than employees whose 
rate of compensation is not greater than 
$960 a year or 40 cents an hour.”
This amendment shall remain in 
effect during the continuance of the 
war in which the United States is now 
engaged and for six months after the 
termination thereof.
F ranklin  D R oosevelt
T he  W hite  H ouse ,
May 18, 1942.
```

```text
# EO:5704: Executive Order 5704. September 2, 1931
# Source: Proclamations and Executive Orders: Herbert Hoover, March 4, 1929 to March 4, 1933, Volume II (Executive Orders 5550-6070; Tables 1-3; Index) (Washington: U.S. Government Printing Office, 1974 (GPO stock no. 2203-00906)); Google, University of California copy (received Jan 6, 1975); HathiTrust handle 2027/uc1.c025504761

# Text: OCR text layer of the scan (pypdf 6.20.1 extract_text(), NUL characters removed; the layer is Google's OCR), running heads and page numbers removed. Not human-verified. Body sha256 f619885e5ebb4a592dfc504198afbc55bc88c545456651aec3465174f3d382d2

Executive Order 5704. September 2, 1931
Executive Order
Delimiting Canal Zone Judicial Districts
SECTION I. By virtue of the authority vested in me by law, section 1 of Executive
Order No. 1898 of March 12, 1914, relating to the Canal Zone judiciary , as amended
by Executive Order No. 3965 of February 26 , 1924 , is hereby amended to read as
follows :
SECTION 1. The Balboa Division of the District Court of the Canal Zone , in
conformity with the Panama Canal Act, shall include all that part of the Canal Zone
which lies within the lines of the 10-mile zone and extends from the left bank of the
Rio Chagres and the shore line of Gatun Lake, 87 feet above mean sea level , to the
Pacific Ocean , and in addition thereto the following territory, now a part of the Canal
Zone , shown on Panama Canal Drawing No. 6071-1 Revised , having a total area of
approximately 26 square miles :
Beginning at 100 -foot contour monument No. 37%, which monument is a 4-inch
galvanized iron pipe extending about 2 feet above the ground , the geographic position
of which (referred to the Canal Zone triangulation system by stadia traverse ) is in
latitude 9 ° 12 ′ plus 2,644.2 feet and longitude 79 ° 37′ plus 4,793.4 feet , said monu-
ment being on the right bank of the Rio Chagres and the left bank of the Quebrada
Madronal near its mouth ; thence following the 100-foot contour along the left bank
of the Quebrada Madronal upstream to where the 100 -foot contour crosses the Que-
brada Madronal and intersects the right bank of the stream , at concrete monument
No. 37%, in latitude 9° 12 ′ plus 5,664.8 feet and longitude 79 ° 37′ plus 4,005.5 feet ;
thence following the right bank of the Quebrada Madronal upstream to a concrete
monument in latitude 9 ° 14' and longitude 79 ° 36′ plus 5,262.7 feet ; thence east a
distance of 768.3 feet to 260- foot contour monument No. 164 % in latitude 9 ° 14′ and
longitude 79 ° 36′ plus 4,494.4 feet ; thence following the continuous 260-foot contour
around and across the Rio Chagres and its tributaries , the Rio Pequeni and the Rio
Boqueron , through the 260-foot contour monuments , numbered 164 to 20, to 260-foot
contour monument No. 19 %, at the intersection of the 260-foot contour with the left
bank of the Rio Azote Caballo , in latitude 9° 10' plus 4,690.9 feet , and longitude
79 ° 34 ′ plus 5,655.3 feet ; thence N. 85 ° 30′ 20 ″ W. a distance of 3,559.8 feet , crossing
the Rio Chilibrillo , to a concrete monument in latitude 9° 10 ' plus 4,969.8 feet and
longitude 79 ° 35' plus 3,194.6 feet ; thence N. 37 ° 46 ′ E. a distance of 550.0 feet to a
concrete monument on the left bank of the Rio Chilibrillo ; thence following the left
bank of the Rio Chilibrillo downstream to a concrete monument in latitude 9° 11′
plus 1,142.1 feet and longitude 79 ° 36′ plus 4,860.4 feet ; thence S. 56 ° 06' W. a distance
of 300.1 feet , crossing the Madden Road at its center -line station 549 plus 68.9 feet ,
to a concrete monument which is 137.5 feet from the center line of said Madden Road ;
thence paralleling the Madden Road , in a northerly direction and at a distance of
137.5 feet from the center line thereof , to a concrete monument in latitude 9° 11′ plus
4,823.6 feet and longitude 79 ° 37′ plus 194.8 feet ; thence N. 75° 45′ W. a distance of
1,547.1 feet to a concrete monument in the Quebrada Moja Polla ; thence down the
Quebrada Moja Polla to the crossing of the 100-foot contour at monument No. 33 ;
thence following the 100-foot contour through monuments Nos . 34 and 35 in a westerly ,
northerly , and easterly direction to a point on the left bank of the Rio Chagres , directly
south of the point of beginning ; thence due north across the Rio Chagres to the point
ofbeginning;
and the Cristobal Division of said court shall include all of the territory within the
lines of the 10-mile zone extending from the Balboa Division to the Atlantic Ocean ,
and the area of Gatun Lake beyond the lines of the 10-mile zone up to the contour
line of 100 feet above mean sea level and the islands in said lake; and the peninsulas
bordering on said lake which have been taken by the United States for the purposes
of the Panama Canal , with the exception of the area along the Rio Chagres , upstream
from the mouth of the Quebrada Madronal , which area is in the Balboa District .
References to right or left banks of rivers relate to the location when facing downstream .
SECTION II . This order shall take effect and be in force from and after the date
hereof.
THE WHITE HOUSE ,
September 2, 1931 .
HERBERT HOOVER
[No. 5704]

```
